# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations  'opposite: flight
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight  'opposite: reservations
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations  'opposite: flight
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight  'opposite: reservations
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::confirm
Summary: Confirms a pending reservation identified by its ID, provided the flight has not departed
         and is still open for booking.
Algorithm:
  1. Search all bookings of this customer for a Reservation with id = reservationID.
     If not found, return false.
  2. Let r be the found reservation.
  3. If r.flight.openForBooking == false, return false.
  4. If now >= r.flight.departureTime, return false.
  5. Set r.status = CONFIRMED.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::reservations, Reservation::status, Reservation::flight,
                 Flight::openForBooking, Flight::departureTime.
Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: reservationID : String — unique identifier of the reservation to confirm.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if confirmation was successful; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: reservationID corresponds to a Reservation belonging to this customer.
Post-condition: If successful, the reservation's status is CONFIRMED.

Customer::cancel
Summary: Cancels an existing reservation identified by its ID, provided the flight has not departed
         and is still open for booking.
Algorithm:
  1. Search all bookings of this customer for a Reservation with id = reservationID.
     If not found, return false.
  2. Let r be the found reservation.
  3. If r.flight.openForBooking == false, return false.
  4. If now >= r.flight.departureTime, return false.
  5. Set r.status = CANCELED.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::reservations, Reservation::status, Reservation::flight,
                 Flight::openForBooking, Flight::departureTime.
Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: reservationID : String — unique identifier of the reservation to cancel.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if cancellation was successful; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: reservationID corresponds to a Reservation belonging to this customer.
Post-condition: If successful, the reservation's status is CANCELED.

Customer::addBooking
Summary: Creates a booking for a customer on a specific open flight for a list of passengers.
         Validates no duplicate passenger names on the same flight and that departure time is in the future.
Algorithm:
  1. If now >= f.departureTime, return false.
  2. If f.openForBooking == false, return false.
  3. For each passenger name in listOfPassengerNames:
       if passenger name already exists in any reservation on f (check f.reservations), return false.
  4. Create a new Booking object b with this customer as owner.
  5. For each passenger name in listOfPassengerNames:
       call b.createReservation(f, passengerName, now).
       If any call returns false, rollback (remove all created reservations) and return false.
  6. Add b to this.bookings list.
  7. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::createReservation, Flight::reservations, Flight::openForBooking,
                 Flight::departureTime, Passenger::name.
Related Classes: Booking, Flight, Reservation, Passenger, EDate
Input: f : Flight — the flight to book.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
       listOfPassengerNames : List<String> — list of passenger names (non-empty, no duplicates within list).
Output: boolean — true if booking was created successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: f is not null, listOfPassengerNames is non-empty and contains no duplicate names.
Post-condition: If successful, a new Booking is added to this.bookings, containing one PENDING Reservation per passenger.

```plantuml
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations  'opposite: flight
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight  'opposite: reservations
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::confirm
Summary: Confirms a pending reservation identified by its ID, provided the flight has not departed
         and is still open for booking.
Algorithm:
  1. Search all bookings of this customer for a Reservation with id = reservationID.
     If not found, return false.
  2. Let r be the found reservation.
  3. If r.flight.openForBooking == false, return false.
  4. If now >= r.flight.departureTime, return false.
  5. Set r.status = CONFIRMED.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::reservations, Reservation::status, Reservation::flight,
                 Flight::openForBooking, Flight::departureTime.
Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: reservationID : String — unique identifier of the reservation to confirm.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if confirmation was successful; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: reservationID corresponds to a Reservation belonging to this customer.
Post-condition: If successful, the reservation's status is CONFIRMED.

Customer::cancel
Summary: Cancels an existing reservation identified by its ID, provided the flight has not departed
         and is still open for booking.
Algorithm:
  1. Search all bookings of this customer for a Reservation with id = reservationID.
     If not found, return false.
  2. Let r be the found reservation.
  3. If r.flight.openForBooking == false, return false.
  4. If now >= r.flight.departureTime, return false.
  5. Set r.status = CANCELED.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::reservations, Reservation::status, Reservation::flight,
                 Flight::openForBooking, Flight::departureTime.
Related Classes: Booking, Reservation, Flight, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: reservationID : String — unique identifier of the reservation to cancel.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if cancellation was successful; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: reservationID corresponds to a Reservation belonging to this customer.
Post-condition: If successful, the reservation's status is CANCELED.

Customer::addBooking
Summary: Creates a booking for a customer on a specific open flight for a list of passengers.
         Validates no duplicate passenger names on the same flight and that departure time is in the future.
Algorithm:
  1. If now >= f.departureTime, return false.
  2. If f.openForBooking == false, return false.
  3. For each passenger name in listOfPassengerNames:
       if passenger name already exists in any reservation on f (check f.reservations), return false.
  4. Create a new Booking object b with this customer as owner.
  5. For each passenger name in listOfPassengerNames:
       call b.createReservation(f, passengerName, now).
       If any call returns false, rollback (remove all created reservations) and return false.
  6. Add b to this.bookings list.
  7. Return true.
Related Features: bookings list, Booking::createReservation, Flight::reservations, Flight::openForBooking,
                 Flight::departureTime, Passenger::name.
Related Classes: Booking, Flight, Reservation, Passenger, EDate
Input: f : Flight — the flight to book.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
       listOfPassengerNames : List<String> — list of passenger names (non-empty, no duplicates within list).
Output: boolean — true if booking was created successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: f is not null, listOfPassengerNames is non-empty and contains no duplicate names.
Post-condition: If successful, a new Booking is added to this.bookings, containing one PENDING Reservation per passenger.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Operation Specifications
Flight::getConfirmedReservations
Summary: Retrieves all confirmed reservations for this flight, provided the flight is open for booking.
Algorithm:
  1. If openForBooking == false, return empty list.
  2. Create an empty result list.
  3. For each Reservation r in reservations:
       if r.status == CONFIRMED, add r to result list.
  4. Return result list.
Related Features: reservations list, openForBooking, Reservation::status.
Related Classes: Reservation, ReservationStatus
Input: None.
Output: List<Reservation> — list of confirmed reservations (may be empty).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Returns confirmed reservations only if the flight is open; otherwise empty list.

Flight::removeStopover
Summary: Removes a stopover from a flight, provided the stopover's arrival time is in the future.
Algorithm:
  1. If stop is not in stopovers list, return false.
  2. If now >= stop.arrivalTime, return false. (Cannot remove past stopovers)
  3. Remove stop from stopovers list.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: stopovers list.
Related Classes: Stopover, EDate
Input: stop : Stopover — the stopover to remove.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if stopover was removed successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: stop is in the stopovers list.
Post-condition: If successful, stopovers no longer contains stop.

Flight::addStopover
Summary: Adds a stopover to a flight, provided the stopover's times are consistent with the flight schedule.
Algorithm:
  1. If now >= stop.arrivalTime, return false. (Stopover must be in the future)
  2. If stop.departureTime >= stop.arrivalTime, return false.
  3. If there is an existing stopover at the same airport with overlapping times, return false.
  4. Add stop to stopovers list.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: stopovers list, Flight::departureTime, Flight::arrivalTime.
Related Classes: Stopover, Airport, EDate
Input: stop : Stopover — the stopover to add.
       now : EDate — current timestamp.
Output: boolean — true if stopover was added successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: stop is not null and its airport is not null.
Post-condition: If successful, stopovers contains the new stopover.

The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```text
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Operation Specifications
Airline::publishFlight
Summary: Publishes a flight to make it available for booking. Validates that the flight has not been published before (openForBooking is false), timestamps are in valid format, currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime, and departureAirport != arrivalAirport. Sets openForBooking to true on success. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If f is null, return false.
  2. If f.openForBooking is true, return false.
  3. If f.departureTime or f.arrivalTime is null or not in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, return false.
  4. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
  5. If f.departureTime is not before f.arrivalTime, return false.
  6. If f.departureAirport is null or f.arrivalAirport is null or f.departureAirport == f.arrivalAirport, return false.
  7. Set f.openForBooking to true.
  8. Return true.
Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute.
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
Input: f : Flight — the flight to publish; now : EDate — the current date/time.
Output: boolean — true if publication succeeded, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: f is not null, f.openForBooking is false, timestamps are valid and satisfy now < f.departureTime < f.arrivalTime, departureAirport != arrivalAirport.
Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to true. If returns false, no changes are made.

Airline::searchFlights
Summary: Searches for flights matching given origin airport id, destination airport id, and departure date.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty result list.
  2. For each Flight f in flights:
       if f.departureAirport.id == origin
          AND f.arrivalAirport.id == dest
          AND f.departureTime.date == date.date
          AND f.openForBooking == true:
            add f to result list.
  3. Return result list.
Related Features: flights list, Flight::departureAirport, Flight::arrivalAirport, Flight::departureTime,
                 Flight::openForBooking.
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
Input: origin : String — departure airport id.
       dest : String — arrival airport id.
       date : EDate — desired departure date.
Output: List<Flight> — list of matching flights (may be empty).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Returns all open flights matching the given criteria.

Airline::removeFlight
Summary: Removes a Flight object from the airline's list of flights.
Algorithm:
  1. Locate f in the flights list.
  2. If found, remove it from the list.
Related Features: flights list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight — the flight to remove (must be present in flights list).
Output: void
Pre-condition: f is present in the flights list.
Post-condition: flights no longer contains f.

Airline::addFlight
Summary: Adds a new Flight object to the airline's list of flights.
Algorithm:
  1. Receive a Flight object f.
  2. Append f to the flights list.
     (No validation is required; validation occurs at publish time.)
Related Features: flights list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight — the flight to be added (must be non-null).
Output: void
Pre-condition: f is not null and is not already present in the flights list.
Post-condition: flights contains f.

Airline::closeFlight
Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed. Verifies the flight is currently open, then changes status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. Search this.flights for a flight with id equal to flightId.
  2. If not found, return false.
  3. Let f be the found flight.
  4. If f.openForBooking is false, return false.
  5. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
  6. If now has the same calendar date (year-month-day) as f.departureTime, return false.
  7. Set f.openForBooking to false.
  8. For each reservation r in f.reservations:
       a. If r.status == CONFIRMED, set r.status to CANCELED.
  9. Return true.
Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute, Flight.reservations list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: flightId : String — the unique ID of the flight to close; now : EDate — the current date/time.
Output: boolean — true if closing succeeded, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: A flight with the given ID exists in this.flights, f.openForBooking is true, now is before f.departureTime, and now is not on the same calendar date as f.departureTime.
Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to false, and every reservation in f.reservations with status CONFIRMED is changed to CANCELED. If returns false, no changes are made.

# Requirement
```text
Airlines offer different flights. Each flight has its unique id. A flight is open for booking and closed by order of the company. A customer can book one or more flights, for different passengers. A reservation concerns a single flight and a single passenger, has a unique id. A reservation can be confirmed or canceled. A flight has a departure airport and an arrival airport. Each airport has its unique id. A flight has a day and time of departure, and a day and time of arrival (e.g. 2024-12-08 13:00:00). A flight may include stopovers in one or more airport(s). A stopover has a departure time and an arrival time. Each airport serves one or more cities.
Functional requirement:
1.Publish a flight for booking. An airline can publish a newly created flight to make it available for customer bookings. The system first confirms that the flight has valid departure and arrival timestamps in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, then checks temporal consistency — currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime — and route integrity — departureAirport ≠ arrivalAirport. A flight may be published only once, and before publishing, the flight's openForBooking status must be false. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
2.Create a booking for passengers on a flight. A customer selects a specific open flight and supplies a list of passengers names. The system checks that there are no duplicate passengers on the flight and the current time is before the flight departure time. It then generates a booking for the customer and creates a reservation with a unique ID for each passenger in that booking. Each reservation is initially placed in "pending" status until the customer confirms it. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3.Confirm or cancel an existing reservation in a booking. The system checks that the flight has not yet departed and is still open for booking. The customer can then confirm or cancel a reservation by providing its reservation ID. The call returns true on success; otherwise, false.
4.Close an open flight. An airline can close an existing flight that has not yet departed. The system verifies that the flight is currently open; if so, it changes the status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure — even if the current time is before the departure time. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
5.Retrieve all confirmed reservations for a specific flight. Given a flight object, the system retrieves every confirmed reservation on that flight, provided the flight is currently open for booking. It returns a list of reservations, or empty list if none exist.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title flights Model

class Airline {
    flights : List<Flight>
    addFlight(f : Flight) : void
    closeFlight(flightId : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    publishFlight(f : Flight, now : EDate) : boolean
    removeFlight(f : Flight) : void
    searchFlights(origin : String, dest : String, date : EDate) : List<Flight>
}
class Airport {
    id : String
    servesForCities : List<City>
    addCity(c : City) : void
}
class Booking {
    customer : Customer
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    createReservation(f : Flight, passenger : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class City {
}
class Customer {
    bookings : List<Booking>
    addBooking(f : Flight, now : EDate, listOfPassengerNames : List<String>) : boolean
    cancel(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
    confirm(reservationID : String, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Flight {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    id : String
    openForBooking : boolean
    arrivalAirport : Airport
    departureAirport : Airport
    reservations : List<Reservation>
    stopovers : List<Stopover>
    addStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
    getConfirmedReservations() : List<Reservation>
    removeStopover(stop : Stopover, now : EDate) : boolean
}
class Passenger {
    name : String
}
class Reservation {
    id : String
    status : ReservationStatus
    flight : Flight
    passenger : Passenger
}
class Stopover {
    arrivalTime : EDate
    departureTime : EDate
    airport : Airport
}
enum ReservationStatus {
    PENDING
    CONFIRMED
    CANCELED
}

Airline *-- "*" Flight : flights
Airport --> "*" City : servesForCities
Booking --> Customer : customer
Booking *-- "*" Reservation : reservations
Customer --> "*" Booking : bookings
Flight --> Airport : arrivalAirport
Flight --> Airport : departureAirport
Flight --> "*" Reservation : reservations  'opposite: flight
Flight *-- "*" Stopover : stopovers
Reservation --> Flight : flight  'opposite: reservations
Reservation --> Passenger : passenger
Stopover --> Airport : airport
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Airline::publishFlight
Summary: Publishes a flight to make it available for booking. Validates that the flight has not been published before (openForBooking is false), timestamps are in valid format, currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime, and departureAirport != arrivalAirport. Sets openForBooking to true on success. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If f is null, return false.
  2. If f.openForBooking is true, return false.
  3. If f.departureTime or f.arrivalTime is null or not in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, return false.
  4. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
  5. If f.departureTime is not before f.arrivalTime, return false.
  6. If f.departureAirport is null or f.arrivalAirport is null or f.departureAirport == f.arrivalAirport, return false.
  7. Set f.openForBooking to true.
  8. Return true.
Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute.
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
Input: f : Flight — the flight to publish; now : EDate — the current date/time.
Output: boolean — true if publication succeeded, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: f is not null, f.openForBooking is false, timestamps are valid and satisfy now < f.departureTime < f.arrivalTime, departureAirport != arrivalAirport.
Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to true. If returns false, no changes are made.

Airline::searchFlights
Summary: Searches for flights matching given origin airport id, destination airport id, and departure date.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty result list.
  2. For each Flight f in flights:
       if f.departureAirport.id == origin
          AND f.arrivalAirport.id == dest
          AND f.departureTime.date == date.date
          AND f.openForBooking == true:
            add f to result list.
  3. Return result list.
Related Features: flights list, Flight::departureAirport, Flight::arrivalAirport, Flight::departureTime,
                 Flight::openForBooking.
Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
Input: origin : String — departure airport id.
       dest : String — arrival airport id.
       date : EDate — desired departure date.
Output: List<Flight> — list of matching flights (may be empty).
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Returns all open flights matching the given criteria.

Airline::removeFlight
Summary: Removes a Flight object from the airline's list of flights.
Algorithm:
  1. Locate f in the flights list.
  2. If found, remove it from the list.
Related Features: flights list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight — the flight to remove (must be present in flights list).
Output: void
Pre-condition: f is present in the flights list.
Post-condition: flights no longer contains f.

Airline::addFlight
Summary: Adds a new Flight object to the airline's list of flights.
Algorithm:
  1. Receive a Flight object f.
  2. Append f to the flights list.
     (No validation is required; validation occurs at publish time.)
Related Features: flights list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight
Input: f : Flight — the flight to be added (must be non-null).
Output: void
Pre-condition: f is not null and is not already present in the flights list.
Post-condition: flights contains f.

Airline::closeFlight
Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed. Verifies the flight is currently open, then changes status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. Search this.flights for a flight with id equal to flightId.
  2. If not found, return false.
  3. Let f be the found flight.
  4. If f.openForBooking is false, return false.
  5. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
  6. If now has the same calendar date (year-month-day) as f.departureTime, return false.
  7. Set f.openForBooking to false.
  8. For each reservation r in f.reservations:
       a. If r.status == CONFIRMED, set r.status to CANCELED.
  9. Return true.
Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute, Flight.reservations list attribute.
Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus, EDate
Input: flightId : String — the unique ID of the flight to close; now : EDate — the current date/time.
Output: boolean — true if closing succeeded, false otherwise.
Pre-condition: A flight with the given ID exists in this.flights, f.openForBooking is true, now is before f.departureTime, and now is not on the same calendar date as f.departureTime.
Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to false, and every reservation in f.reservations with status CONFIRMED is changed to CANCELED. If returns false, no changes are made.

# Operation Specifications
Booking::createReservation
Summary: Creates a single reservation for a passenger on a given flight, with status PENDING.
         This is a helper called by Customer::addBooking for each passenger.
Algorithm:
  1. Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID).
  2. Create a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
  3. Create a new Reservation object with:
       id = generated ID,
       status = PENDING,
       flight = f,
       passenger = new Passenger(name).
  4. Add the reservation to this.reservations list.
  5. Add the reservation to f.reservations list.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: reservations list (in Booking), Flight::reservations.
Related Classes: Reservation, Passenger, Flight, ReservationStatus
Input: f : Flight — the flight for which the reservation is made.
       passenger : String — name of the passenger.
       now : EDate — current timestamp (used for validation checks outside this method).
Output: boolean — always true if called (validation is done by the caller).
Pre-condition: f is not null, passenger name is non-empty, and the caller has validated all business rules.
Post-condition: A new Reservation with status PENDING is added to both this.reservations and f.reservations.

# Operation Specifications
Booking::createReservation
Summary: Creates a single reservation for a passenger on a given flight, with status PENDING.
         This is a helper called by Customer::addBooking for each passenger.
Algorithm:
  1. Generate a unique reservation ID (e.g., UUID).
  2. Create a new Passenger object with the given passenger name.
  3. Create a new Reservation object with:
       id = generated ID,
       status = PENDING,
       flight = f,
       passenger = new Passenger(name).
  4. Add the reservation to this.reservations list.
  5. Add the reservation to f.reservations list.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: reservations list (in Booking), Flight::reservations.
Related Classes: Reservation, Passenger, Flight, ReservationStatus
Input: f : Flight — the flight for which the reservation is made.
       passenger : String — name of the passenger.
       now : EDate — current timestamp (used for validation checks outside this method).
Output: boolean — always true if called (validation is done by the caller).
Pre-condition: f is not null, passenger name is non-empty, and the caller has validated all business rules.
Post-condition: A new Reservation with status PENDING is added to both this.reservations and f.reservations.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public boolean addStopover(final Stopover stop, final Date now)
  {
    // 1. If now >= stop.arrivalTime, return false.
    if (now != null && stop.getArrivalTime() != null && !now.before(stop.getArrivalTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 2. If stop.departureTime >= stop.arrivalTime, return false.
    if (stop.getDepartureTime() != null && stop.getArrivalTime() != null && !stop.getDepartureTime().before(stop.getArrivalTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 3. If there is an existing stopover at the same airport with overlapping times, return false.
    for (Stopover existing : getStopovers()) {
      if (existing.getAirport() != null && existing.getAirport().equals(stop.getAirport())) {
        // Check overlapping times
        Date eDep = existing.getDepartureTime();
        Date eArr = existing.getArrivalTime();
        Date sDep = stop.getDepartureTime();
        Date sArr = stop.getArrivalTime();
        if (eDep != null && eArr != null && sDep != null && sArr != null) {
          // Overlap if eDep < sArr AND sDep < eArr
          if (eDep.before(sArr) && sDep.before(eArr)) {
            return false;
          }
        }
      }
    }
    // 4. Add stop to stopovers list.
    getStopovers().add(stop);
    // 5. Return true.
    return true;
  }

          // Overlap if eDep < sArr AND sDep < eArr

  public boolean removeStopover(final Stopover stop, final Date now)
  {
    // 1. If stop is not in stopovers list, return false.
    if (!getStopovers().contains(stop)) {
      return false;
    }
    // 2. If now >= stop.arrivalTime, return false.
    if (now != null && stop.getArrivalTime() != null && !now.before(stop.getArrivalTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 3. Remove stop from stopovers list.
    getStopovers().remove(stop);
    // 4. Return true.
    return true;
  }

  public EList<Reservation> getConfirmedReservations()
  {
    // 1. If this.openForBooking is false, return an empty list.
    if (!isOpenForBooking()) {
      return new BasicEList<Reservation>();
    }
    // 2. Create an empty list result.
    EList<Reservation> result = new BasicEList<Reservation>();
    // 3. For each reservation r in this.reservations:
    for (Reservation r : getReservations()) {
      // a. If r.status == CONFIRMED, add r to result.
      if (r.getStatus() == ReservationStatus.CONFIRMED) {
        result.add(r);
      }
    }
    // 4. Return result.
    return result;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

    // 1. If f is null or not openForBooking, return false.
    if (f == null || !f.isOpenForBooking()) {
      return false;
    }
    // 2. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
    if (!now.before(f.getDepartureTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 3. For each passengerName in listOfPassengerNames:
    for (String passengerName : listOfPassengerNames) {
      // a. If passengerName is null or empty, return false.
      if (passengerName == null || passengerName.isEmpty()) {
        return false;
      }
      // b. If there already exists a reservation in f.reservations with passenger.name equal to passengerName, return false.
      for (Reservation existingRes : f.getReservations()) {
        if (existingRes.getPassenger() != null && passengerName.equals(existingRes.getPassenger().getName())) {
          return false;
        }
      }
    }
    // 4. Create a new Booking b with this as customer and empty reservations list.
    Booking b = FlightsFactory.eINSTANCE.createBooking();
    b.setCustomer(this);

      if (!b.createReservation(f, passengerName, now)) {
        return false;
      }
    }
    // 6. Add b to this.bookings.
    getBookings().add(b);
    // 7. Return true.
    return true;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * Summary: Publishes a flight to make it available for booking. Validates that the flight has not been published before (openForBooking is false), timestamps are in valid format, currentTime < departureTime < arrivalTime, and departureAirport != arrivalAirport. Sets openForBooking to true on success. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If f is null, return false.
   *   2. If f.openForBooking is true, return false.
   *   3. If f.departureTime or f.arrivalTime is null or not in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, return false.
   *   4. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
   *   5. If f.departureTime is not before f.arrivalTime, return false.
   *   6. If f.departureAirport is null or f.arrivalAirport is null or f.departureAirport == f.arrivalAirport, return false.
   *   7. Set f.openForBooking to true.
   *   8. Return true.
   * Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute.
   * Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
   * Input: f : Flight — the flight to publish; now : EDate — the current date/time.
   * Output: boolean — true if publication succeeded, false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: f is not null, f.openForBooking is false, timestamps are valid and satisfy now < f.departureTime < f.arrivalTime, departureAirport != arrivalAirport.
   * Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to true. If returns false, no changes are made.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean publishFlight(final Flight f, final Date now)
  {
    // 1. If f is null, return false.
    if (f == null) {
      return false;
    }
    // 2. If f.openForBooking is true, return false.
    if (f.isOpenForBooking()) {
      return false;
    }
    // 3. If f.departureTime or f.arrivalTime is null or not in yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss format, return false.
    SimpleDateFormat sdf = new SimpleDateFormat("yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss");
    sdf.setLenient(false);
    if (f.getDepartureTime() == null || f.getArrivalTime() == null) {
      return false;
    }
    try {
      sdf.format(f.getDepartureTime());
      sdf.format(f.getArrivalTime());
    } catch (Exception e) {
      return false;
    }
    // 4. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
    if (!now.before(f.getDepartureTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 5. If f.departureTime is not before f.arrivalTime, return false.
    if (!f.getDepartureTime().before(f.getArrivalTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 6. If f.departureAirport is null or f.arrivalAirport is null or f.departureAirport == f.arrivalAirport, return false.
    if (f.getDepartureAirport() == null || f.getArrivalAirport() == null || f.getDepartureAirport() == f.getArrivalAirport()) {
      return false;
    }
    // 7. Set f.openForBooking to true.
    f.setOpenForBooking(true);
    // 8. Return true.
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Closes an open flight that has not yet departed. Verifies the flight is currently open, then changes status to closed and cancels every confirmed reservation. Closing is not allowed on the same calendar day as the flight's departure. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Search this.flights for a flight with id equal to flightId.
   *   2. If not found, return false.
   *   3. Let f be the found flight.
   *   4. If f.openForBooking is false, return false.
   *   5. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
   *   6. If now has the same calendar date (year-month-day) as f.departureTime, return false.
   *   7. Set f.openForBooking to false.
   *   8. For each reservation r in f.reservations:
   *        a. If r.status == CONFIRMED, set r.status to CANCELED.
   *   9. Return true.
   * Related Features: flights list attribute, Flight.openForBooking attribute, Flight.reservations list attribute.
   * Related Classes: Flight, Reservation, ReservationStatus, EDate
   * Input: flightId : String — the unique ID of the flight to close; now : EDate — the current date/time.
   * Output: boolean — true if closing succeeded, false otherwise.
   * Pre-condition: A flight with the given ID exists in this.flights, f.openForBooking is true, now is before f.departureTime, and now is not on the same calendar date as f.departureTime.
   * Post-condition: If returns true, f.openForBooking is set to false, and every reservation in f.reservations with status CONFIRMED is changed to CANCELED. If returns false, no changes are made.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean closeFlight(final String flightId, final Date now)
  {
    // 1. Search this.flights for a flight with id equal to flightId.
    Flight foundFlight = null;
    for (Flight f : getFlights()) {
      if (f.getId().equals(flightId)) {
        foundFlight = f;
        break;
      }
    }
    // 2. If not found, return false.
    if (foundFlight == null) {
      return false;
    }
    Flight f = foundFlight;
    // 4. If f.openForBooking is false, return false.
    if (!f.isOpenForBooking()) {
      return false;
    }
    // 5. If now is not before f.departureTime, return false.
    if (!now.before(f.getDepartureTime())) {
      return false;
    }
    // 6. If now has the same calendar date (year-month-day) as f.departureTime, return false.
    Calendar calNow = Calendar.getInstance();
    calNow.setTime(now);
    Calendar calDeparture = Calendar.getInstance();
    calDeparture.setTime(f.getDepartureTime());
    if (calNow.get(Calendar.YEAR) == calDeparture.get(Calendar.YEAR) &&
        calNow.get(Calendar.MONTH) == calDeparture.get(Calendar.MONTH) &&
        calNow.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH) == calDeparture.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH)) {
      return false;
    }
    // 7. Set f.openForBooking to false.
    f.setOpenForBooking(false);
    // 8. For each reservation r in f.reservations:
    //    a. If r.status == CONFIRMED, set r.status to CANCELED.
    for (Reservation r : f.getReservations()) {
      if (r.getStatus() == ReservationStatus.CONFIRMED) {
        r.setStatus(ReservationStatus.CANCELED);
      }
    }
    // 9. Return true.
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Searches for flights matching given origin airport id, destination airport id, and departure date.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize an empty result list.
   *   2. For each Flight f in flights:
   *        if f.departureAirport.id == origin
   *           AND f.arrivalAirport.id == dest
   *           AND f.departureTime.date == date.date
   *           AND f.openForBooking == true:
   *             add f to result list.
   *   3. Return result list.
   * Related Features: flights list, Flight::departureAirport, Flight::arrivalAirport, Flight::departureTime,
   *                  Flight::openForBooking.
   * Related Classes: Flight, Airport, EDate
   * Input: origin : String — departure airport id.
   *        dest : String — arrival airport id.
   *        date : EDate — desired departure date.
   * Output: List<Flight> — list of matching flights (may be empty).
   * Pre-condition: None.
   * Post-condition: Returns all open flights matching the given criteria.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Flight> searchFlights(final String origin, final String dest, final Date date)
  {
    // 1. Initialize an empty result list.
    EList<Flight> result = new BasicEList<Flight>();
    // 2. For each Flight f in flights:
    for (Flight f : getFlights()) {
      if (f.getDepartureAirport() != null && f.getDepartureAirport().getId().equals(origin)
          && f.getArrivalAirport() != null && f.getArrivalAirport().getId().equals(dest)
          && f.getDepartureTime() != null && sameDate(f.getDepartureTime(), date)
          && f.isOpenForBooking()) {
        result.add(f);
      }
    }
    // 3. Return result list.
    return result;
  }

  /**
   * Helper method to check if two dates fall on the same calendar date (year-month-day).
   */
  private boolean sameDate(Date d1, Date d2) {
    Calendar cal1 = Calendar.getInstance();
    cal1.setTime(d1);
    Calendar cal2 = Calendar.getInstance();
    cal2.setTime(d2);
    return cal1.get(Calendar.YEAR) == cal2.get(Calendar.YEAR)
        && cal1.get(Calendar.MONTH) == cal2.get(Calendar.MONTH)
        && cal1.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH) == cal2.get(Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH);
  }

   * Summary: Adds a new Flight object to the airline's list of flights.

   *   1. Receive a Flight object f.
   *   2. Append f to the flights list.
   *      (No validation is required; validation occurs at publish time.)
   * Related Features: flights list attribute.

   * Input: f : Flight — the flight to be added (must be non-null).

   * Pre-condition: f is not null and is not already present in the flights list.
   * Post-condition: flights contains f.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void addFlight(final Flight f)
  {
    getFlights().add(f);
  }

  /**

  public void removeFlight(final Flight f)
